````math
## Appendix

## Appendix A Supplementary Discussion

### A.1 Interpretation of the overlap correction

We do not develop a complete model of validation data reuse in this
work. Detailed studies of adaptive data analysis can be found in prior
work ([Dwork et al., 2015](#bib.bib8); [Rogers et al.,
2016](#bib.bib23); [Russo and Zou, 2016](#bib.bib53); [Xu and Raginsky,
2017](#bib.bib54)). Instead, we state sufficient conditions for valid
e-values and focus on a framework for FER control across RSI rounds. The
interpretation of $`\beta`$ is related to how historical validation
information, including feedback summarized in $`C_{t}`$, is used in
candidate construction and selection. Greater reliance on information
from previously evaluated samples may increase optimism when those
samples are reused. For example, later rounds may use a few failed cases
from the previous round or summaries of common error types. These forms
of feedback reveal different information about the validation data and
may lead to different degrees of optimism bias in subsequent evaluation.
The parameter $`\beta`$ specifies an allowance for this optimism under
the null hypothesis. A stronger correction requires stronger validation
results to reach the acceptance threshold, making the gate more
conservative and potentially limiting both false acceptances and the
acceptance of useful updates. Our sensitivity results in
Section [6.2](#S6.SS2 "6.2 Ablation study ‣ 6 Numerical Experiments ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
and
Appendix [H](#A8 "Appendix H Supplementary Sensitivity Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
support the practical value of an appropriate correction strength. In
the reported ablation, an appropriate $`\beta`$ achieves a higher mean
final gain than no correction, whereas larger values make the gate
overly conservative and reduce final gains.

### A.2 Relation to Existing Statistical Methods

TRUST adapts FDR control in multiple hypothesis testing to the
acceptance of candidates in self-evolving agents. It provides a
framework covering the validation protocol, correction for data reuse,
noninferiority testing, construction of test statistics, and allocation
of testing levels. Within this framework, an LLM analyzes the candidate
and historical context to guide allocation, while statistical guarantees
hold under the stated assumptions. Existing statistical studies ([Zhang
et al., 2025b](#bib.bib43); [Chen and Kasiviswanathan, 2020](#bib.bib6))
compare testing procedures on specified hypotheses and observation
streams in their numerical experiments. In our RSI setting, the proposer
constructs candidates using historical information. Different gates can
produce different acceptance decisions and incumbents, leading to
different histories and subsequent candidates. Our experiments follow
this closed loop and use update operators motivated by prior work on RSI
agents. This allows us to compare how different gates affect both
acceptance decisions and subsequent search on real tasks. Our
theoretical analysis follows the same adaptive process and connects a
lower bound on final gain to the testing levels and other parameters of
the gate. It thus provides a statistical foundation for analyzing both
acceptance risk and improvement along the trajectory induced by the
gate.

## Appendix B Proof of Conditional Evidence Validity

### B.1 Proof of Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")

In each round, the sampling plan is to draw $`n`$ tasks from the fixed
validation pool of size $`N`$, where $`n\leq N`$. All $`N`$ sample
indices are selectable at the start of the round, so
$`\mathcal{R}_{t,0}=\{1,2,\ldots,N\}`$. At draw $`i`$, we select
$`K_{t,i}`$ uniformly from the remaining set $`\mathcal{R}_{t,i-1}`$ and
remove it, giving
$`\mathcal{R}_{t,i}=\mathcal{R}_{t,i-1}\setminus\{K_{t,i}\}`$. Thus, a
sample can be drawn at most once within a round but may be used again in
later rounds.

Let $`L_{t,i}\in\{0,1\}`$ be the $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable
indicator that draw $`i`$ is made.

|     |                                                                    |     |
|-----|--------------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       L_{t,i}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if draw $i$ is made in round $t$},\\
       0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
       ```                                                                 |     |

Within each round, sampling proceeds consecutively and does not continue
after stopping. If $`\alpha_{t}=0`$, no validation samples are drawn.

Each round uses at most $`n`$ samples. The bounds on the betting
fractions in
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")(c)
apply on draws that are made ($`L_{t,i}=1`$). If sampling stops early,
we set the variables for the remaining indices to zero as a notational
convention for the proofs:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
Y_{t,i}=Q_{t,i}=\zeta_{t,i}=\eta_{t,i}=0\qquad\text{when }L_{t,i}=0.
``` |  |

After stopping, no more samples are used, and the history and record of
previously used samples stay unchanged. When a sample is drawn,
$`Q_{t,i}`$ equals one with conditional probability $`\zeta_{t,i}`$.
Hence,

|     |                                                         |     |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \mathbb{E}[Q_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]=\zeta_{t,i}.
       ```                                                      |     |

After stopping, both $`Q_{t,i}`$ and $`\zeta_{t,i}`$ are set to zero, so
this equality still holds.

###### Lemma 1.

Let $`(\mathcal{H}_{i})_{i=0}^{n}`$ be a filtration, $`Y_{i}\in[0,1]`$
be $`\mathcal{H}_{i}`$-measurable, and $`u_{i}\in(0,1]`$ and
$`\eta_{i}\in[0,1)`$ be $`\mathcal{H}_{i-1}`$-measurable for each
$`i=1,\ldots,n`$. If

|     |                                                   |     |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \mathbb{E}[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i-1}]\leq u_{i},
       ```                                                |     |

then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
M_{0}=1,\qquad M_{m}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\left[1+\eta_{i}\frac{Y_{i}-u_{i}}{u_{i}}\right]
``` |  |

is a nonnegative supermartingale. Moreover, for every stopping time
$`\tau\leq n`$, $`\mathbb{E}[M_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}]\leq 1`$.

###### Proof.

Write $`G_{i}=1+\eta_{i}(Y_{i}-u_{i})/u_{i}`$. Since $`Y_{i}\geq 0`$,
$`G_{i}\geq 1-\eta_{i}>0`$. Since $`u_{i}`$ and $`\eta_{i}`$ are
$`\mathcal{H}_{i-1}`$-measurable, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[G_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i-1}]=1+\frac{\eta_{i}}{u_{i}}\left(\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i-1}]-u_{i}\right)\leq 1.
``` |  |

By the product definition, $`M_{i}=M_{i-1}G_{i}`$ and
$`M_{i-1}=\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}G_{j}`$. For $`j<i`$, each $`G_{j}`$ is
$`\mathcal{H}_{j}`$-measurable, with
$`\mathcal{H}_{j}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{i-1}`$. Thus, $`M_{i-1}`$ is
$`\mathcal{H}_{i-1}`$-measurable and can be taken outside the
conditional expectation:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[M_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[M_{i-1}G_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i-1}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=M_{i-1}\mathbb{E}[G_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i-1}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=M_{i-1}\left[1+\frac{\eta_{i}}{u_{i}}\left(\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i-1}]-u_{i}\right)\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq M_{i-1}.`$ |  |

Taking expectations and iterating from $`M_{0}=1`$ gives
$`\mathbb{E}[M_{i}]\leq 1`$, so each $`M_{i}`$ is integrable. Together
with nonnegativity and $`\mathcal{H}_{i}`$-measurability, this proves
the supermartingale claim.

For bounded $`\tau`$, use the finite pathwise decomposition

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
M_{\tau}=M_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{\tau\geq i\}(M_{i}-M_{i-1}).
``` |  |

The event $`\{\tau\geq i\}=\{\tau\leq i-1\}^{c}`$ belongs to
$`\mathcal{H}_{i-1}`$. Hence every stopped increment has nonpositive
conditional mean:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{1}\{\tau\geq i\}(M_{i}-M_{i-1})\middle|\mathcal{H}_{i-1}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbf{1}\{\tau\geq i\}\left(\mathbb{E}[M_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{i-1}]-M_{i-1}\right)\leq 0.`$ |  |

Since $`\mathcal{H}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{i-1}`$, the tower property
implies that each stopped increment also has nonpositive conditional
expectation given $`\mathcal{H}_{0}`$. Taking conditional expectations
given $`\mathcal{H}_{0}`$ in the decomposition of $`M_{\tau}`$ above, we
obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[M_{\tau}\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=M_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{1}\{\tau\geq i\}(M_{i}-M_{i-1})\mid\mathcal{H}_{0}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq M_{0}=1.`$ |  |

∎

###### Proof of Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").

Fix outer round $`t`$. Recall that

|  |  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle Y_{t,i}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1+D_{t,i}}{2},`$ | $`\displaystyle\Delta_{t}`$ | $`\displaystyle=J(\widetilde{\Theta}_{t})-J(\Theta_{t-1}),`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle Q_{t,i}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}\{K_{t,i}\in\mathcal{I}_{t,i-1}\},`$ | $`\displaystyle\zeta_{t,i}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(Q_{t,i}=1\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}).`$ |  |

Both $`\mathcal{R}_{t,i-1}`$ and $`\mathcal{I}_{t,i-1}`$ are
$`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable. Uniform sampling from the remaining
set gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Q_{t,i}=1\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{R}_{t,i-1}}\mathbf{1}\{k\in\mathcal{I}_{t,i-1}\}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{R}_{t,i-1}|}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{|\mathcal{I}_{t,i-1}\cap\mathcal{R}_{t,i-1}|}{|\mathcal{R}_{t,i-1}|}=\zeta_{t,i}.`$ |  |

We bound the contributions of unexposed and reused samples separately.
The history depends only on accessed sample information and
randomization independent of unexposed sample values. Since the pool is
i.i.d., unexposed samples retain distribution $`\mathcal{D}`$
conditional on $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$. Thus, for each draw,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}(1-Q_{t,i})\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]=(1-\zeta_{t,i})\frac{1+\Delta_{t}}{2}.
``` |  | (17) |

Here, $`1-Q_{t,i}`$ retains only the unexposed contribution, and
$`1-\zeta_{t,i}`$ is the probability of drawing an unexposed sample.

For reused samples,
Assumption [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
allows an additional $`\beta`$ above the null boundary
$`p_{\varepsilon}=(1-\varepsilon)/2`$. On null histories with
$`\zeta_{t,i}>0`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}Q_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\zeta_{t,i}\,\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1},Q_{t,i}=1]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\zeta_{t,i}(p_{\varepsilon}+\beta).`$ |  |

When $`\zeta_{t,i}=0`$, conditional monotonicity instead gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
0\leq\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}Q_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]\leq\mathbb{E}[Q_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]=0,
``` |  |

so the same weighted bound holds. Combining this bound with
Equation ([17](#A2.E17 "In Proof of Theorem . ‣ B.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix B Proof of Conditional Evidence Validity ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
and $`(1+\Delta_{t})/2\leq p_{\varepsilon}`$ on $`H_{t}^{0}`$ yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}Q_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]+\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}(1-Q_{t,i})\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\zeta_{t,i}(p_{\varepsilon}+\beta)+(1-\zeta_{t,i})\frac{1+\Delta_{t}}{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq p_{\varepsilon}+\zeta_{t,i}\beta.`$ |  |

The correction $`\zeta_{t,i}\beta`$ therefore weights the overlap
correction by the probability of drawing a reused sample. This also
covers $`\zeta_{t,i}=1`$, when the unexposed contribution vanishes.

When draw $`i`$ is not made, $`L_{t,i}=0`$ and $`Y_{t,i}=Q_{t,i}=0`$ by
the convention above. Since $`L_{t,i}`$ is
$`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable, the indicator
$`\mathbf{1}\{L_{t,i}=0\}`$ is also $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable
and can be taken outside the conditional expectation. Hence,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\{L_{t,i}=0\}\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{L_{t,i}=0\}Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]=0,`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\{L_{t,i}=0\}\mathbb{E}[Q_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}\{L_{t,i}=0\}Q_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]=0.`$ |  |

On $`\{L_{t,i}=0\}`$, the indicator equals one, so
$`\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]=0`$ and
$`\zeta_{t,i}=\mathbb{E}[Q_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]=0`$.

For draws that are made, the bounds for the unexposed and reused
contributions together give
$`\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]\leq u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}`$ on
$`H_{t}^{0}`$. For draws that are not made, either after early stopping
or because $`\alpha_{t}=0`$, the zero conditional mean above is also at
most $`u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}=p_{\varepsilon}>0`$. Recall that
$`N_{t}^{\varepsilon}=\mathbf{1}\{\Delta_{t}\leq-\varepsilon\}`$. On
$`H_{t}^{0}`$, multiplying by $`N_{t}^{\varepsilon}=1`$ preserves these
bounds; outside $`H_{t}^{0}`$, multiplying by $`N_{t}^{\varepsilon}=0`$
makes both sides zero. Therefore, on all histories,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]\leq N_{t}^{\varepsilon}u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}.
``` |  |

The corrected boundary $`u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}`$ is
$`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable and lies in $`[p_{\varepsilon},1]`$,
with $`p_{\varepsilon}>0`$. Write
$`G_{t,i}=1-\eta_{t,i}+\eta_{t,i}Y_{t,i}/u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}`$. When draw
$`i`$ is not made, we set $`\eta_{t,i}=0`$, so $`G_{t,i}=1`$. For every
$`i`$, the bounds $`0\leq Y_{t,i}\leq 1`$, $`0\leq\eta_{t,i}<1`$, and
$`u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}\geq p_{\varepsilon}>0`$ give

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle G_{t,i}`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq 1-\eta_{t,i}>0,`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle G_{t,i}`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq 1-\eta_{t,i}+\frac{\eta_{t,i}}{p_{\varepsilon}}=1+\eta_{t,i}(p_{\varepsilon}^{-1}-1)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq p_{\varepsilon}^{-1}.`$ |  |

The last inequality uses $`\eta_{t,i}\leq 1`$ and
$`p_{\varepsilon}^{-1}-1\geq 0`$. Hence
$`0<G_{t,i}\leq p_{\varepsilon}^{-1}`$ almost surely.

Since each $`G_{t,i}`$ is $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i}`$-measurable, the product
$`M^{(\beta)}_{t,m}`$ is $`\mathcal{H}_{t,m}`$-measurable. For
$`0\leq m\leq n\leq N`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
0\leq N_{t}^{\varepsilon}M^{(\beta)}_{t,m}\leq M^{(\beta)}_{t,m}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}G_{t,i}\leq p_{\varepsilon}^{-m}\leq p_{\varepsilon}^{-N}.
``` |  |

Since $`p_{\varepsilon}^{-N}`$ is a finite deterministic constant, both
$`M^{(\beta)}_{t,m}`$ and $`N_{t}^{\varepsilon}M^{(\beta)}_{t,m}`$ have
finite expectations and are therefore integrable. After sampling stops,
all remaining factors $`G_{t,i}`$ equal one, so both
$`M^{(\beta)}_{t,m}`$ and $`N_{t}^{\varepsilon}M^{(\beta)}_{t,m}`$
remain unchanged.

Set $`X_{m}=N_{t}^{\varepsilon}M^{(\beta)}_{t,m}`$. Since
$`N_{t}^{\varepsilon}`$ is $`\mathcal{H}_{t,0}`$-measurable, $`X_{m}`$
is $`\mathcal{H}_{t,m}`$-measurable; the bounds above also show that
$`X_{m}`$ is nonnegative and integrable. To establish that
$`(X_{m})_{m=0}^{n}`$ is a supermartingale, it remains to prove

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[X_{m+1}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,m}]\leq X_{m},\qquad m=0,\ldots,n-1.
``` |  |

Since $`\mathcal{H}_{t,0}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$,
$`N_{t}^{\varepsilon}`$ is also $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable. The
terms $`1-\eta_{t,i}`$ and $`\eta_{t,i}/u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}`$ are
$`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable and bounded as well. We can therefore
take these quantities outside the conditional expectation. Using the
bound
$`N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]\leq N_{t}^{\varepsilon}u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}`$
established above, we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[N_{t}^{\varepsilon}G_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}[G_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=N_{t}^{\varepsilon}(1-\eta_{t,i})+\frac{\eta_{t,i}}{u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}}N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq N_{t}^{\varepsilon}(1-\eta_{t,i})+\frac{\eta_{t,i}}{u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}}N_{t}^{\varepsilon}u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=N_{t}^{\varepsilon}.`$ |  |

The product before draw $`i`$ is
$`M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}=\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}G_{t,j}\geq 0`$. For $`j<i`$,
each $`G_{t,j}`$ is $`\mathcal{H}_{t,j}`$-measurable, with
$`\mathcal{H}_{t,j}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$. Thus
$`M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}`$ is $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable and can
also be taken outside the conditional expectation. Since
$`X_{i}=M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}N_{t}^{\varepsilon}G_{t,i}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}N_{t}^{\varepsilon}G_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}\mathbb{E}[N_{t}^{\varepsilon}G_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}N_{t}^{\varepsilon}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=X_{i-1}.`$ |  |

Taking $`i=m+1`$ gives the required conditional inequality. Together
with the nonnegativity, measurability, and integrability established
above, this proves that $`(X_{m})_{m=0}^{n}`$ is a nonnegative
supermartingale. Since $`M^{(\beta)}_{t,0}=1`$, its initial value is
$`X_{0}=N_{t}^{\varepsilon}`$.

For a stopping time $`\tau_{t}\leq n`$, the actual stopped process
$`X_{m\wedge\tau_{t}}`$ is also a supermartingale: its next increment is
$`\mathbf{1}\{\tau_{t}>m\}(X_{m+1}-X_{m})`$, with a predictable
indicator and nonpositive conditional mean. Iterating its conditional
inequality from 0 to $`n`$ yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[X_{n\wedge\tau_{t}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,0}]\leq X_{0}=N_{t}^{\varepsilon},\qquad N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}[M^{(\beta)}_{t,\tau_{t}}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]\leq N_{t}^{\varepsilon}.
``` |  |

On $`H_{t}^{0}`$, we have $`N_{t}^{\varepsilon}=1`$. Therefore, for
every stopping time $`\tau_{t}\leq n`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[M^{(\beta)}_{t,\tau_{t}}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]\leq 1\qquad\text{almost surely on }H_{t}^{0}.
``` |  |

This is the stopping extension of
Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").
In particular, taking $`\tau_{t}=n`$ gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[M^{(\beta)}_{t,n}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]\leq 1\qquad\text{almost surely on }H_{t}^{0},
``` |  |

which is
Equation ([12](#S5.E12 "In Theorem 1. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")).
∎

### B.2 Stopping times

###### Proposition 1.

Suppose $`\alpha_{t}\in(0,1)`$ is
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable and set

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\tau_{t}=\inf\left\{m\in\{0,\ldots,n\}:M^{(\beta)}_{t,m}\geq\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}}\right\}\wedge n.
``` |  |

Then $`\tau_{t}`$ is a stopping time bounded by $`n`$, and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{1}\{M^{(\beta)}_{t,\tau_{t}}\geq 1/\alpha_{t}\}=\mathbf{1}\left\{\max_{0\leq m\leq n}M^{(\beta)}_{t,m}\geq 1/\alpha_{t}\right\}.
``` |  |

###### Proof.

For $`0\leq m<n`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\{\tau_{t}\leq m\}=\bigcup_{j=0}^{m}\{M^{(\beta)}_{t,j}\geq 1/\alpha_{t}\}\in\mathcal{H}_{t,m},
``` |  |

because $`M^{(\beta)}_{t,j}`$ is $`\mathcal{H}_{t,j}`$-measurable,
$`\alpha_{t}`$ is
$`\mathcal{H}_{t,0}=\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable, and
$`\mathcal{H}_{t,0}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{t,j}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{t,m}`$
for every $`j\leq m`$.

By definition, $`\tau_{t}\leq n`$ for every sample outcome. Therefore,
when $`m\geq n`$, we have $`\tau_{t}\leq n\leq m`$ for every outcome, so

|     |                            |     |
|-----|----------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \{\tau_{t}\leq m\}=\Omega,
       ```                         |     |

where $`\Omega`$ is the set of all possible sample outcomes. In
particular, $`\{\tau_{t}\leq n\}=\Omega\in\mathcal{H}_{t,n}`$. Together
with the case $`m<n`$ above, this proves that $`\tau_{t}`$ is a stopping
time bounded by $`n`$.

If a crossing occurs, the stopped value is the first crossing value;
otherwise the process stops at $`n`$ below threshold. ∎

### B.3 Mixtures

#### Betting fractions.

Betting interprets an evidence process as capital accumulated from an
initial value of one ([Waudby-Smith and Ramdas, 2024](#bib.bib36)). In
Equation ([10](#S4.E10 "In 4.2 E-Value Construction with Overlap Correction ‣ 4 Trustworthy Recursive Updates with Statistical Testing ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")),
the amount bet on observation $`i`$ is
$`\eta_{t,i}M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}`$, where $`M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}`$ is the
current capital and $`\eta_{t,i}\in[0,1)`$ specifies the proportion of
that capital put at risk. The new capital equals the current capital
plus the gain or loss from this bet:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle M^{(\beta)}_{t,i}`$ | $`\displaystyle=M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}+\eta_{t,i}M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}\frac{Y_{t,i}-u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}}{u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}\left[(1-\eta_{t,i})+\eta_{t,i}\frac{Y_{t,i}}{u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}}\right].`$ |  |

The uncommitted capital is retained, and the amount bet is multiplied by
the return factor $`Y_{t,i}/u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}`$. Thus $`\eta_{t,i}=0`$
leaves the evidence unchanged; if $`Y_{t,i}=0`$, the update loses
exactly the fraction $`\eta_{t,i}`$ of current capital. Larger betting
fractions increase both the gain when $`Y_{t,i}>u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}`$ and
the loss when $`Y_{t,i}<u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}`$. The amount of each bet is
calculated using the current capital. A betting strategy specifies how
to choose these fractions from the available history: each
$`\eta_{t,i}`$ must be $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable, so it is
chosen before observing $`Y_{t,i}`$.

#### Combining betting strategies.

Let $`L\geq 1`$ be a fixed integer denoting the number of betting
strategies included in the mixture. For each $`\ell\in\{1,\ldots,L\}`$,
strategy $`\ell`$ uses $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable fractions
$`\eta_{t,i}^{(\ell)}\in[0,1)`$ and produces

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
M_{t,0}^{(\beta,\ell)}=1,\qquad M_{t,m}^{(\beta,\ell)}=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\left[1+\eta_{t,i}^{(\ell)}\frac{Y_{t,i}-u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}}{u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}}\right].
``` |  |

All $`L`$ processes use the same validation observations and corrected
boundaries, with potentially different betting fractions. A mixture
combines their evidence by taking a weighted average, allowing several
betting strategies to contribute to the test ([Waudby-Smith and Ramdas,
2024](#bib.bib36)). The weight $`\pi_{t,\ell}`$ is the fraction of
initial capital assigned to strategy $`\ell`$; the weights are
nonnegative, sum to one, are
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable, and remain fixed
throughout the round. Convex averaging preserves e-value validity even
when the component e-values depend on the same observations ([Vovk and
Wang, 2021](#bib.bib33)). The following corollary establishes the
corresponding conditional validity for these sequential processes.

###### Corollary 1.

Let $`M_{t,m}^{(\beta,1)},\ldots,M_{t,m}^{(\beta,L)}`$ use predictable
betting sequences and let nonnegative weights
$`\pi_{t,1},\ldots,\pi_{t,L}`$ be
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable with
$`\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\pi_{t,\ell}=1`$. Then
$`M_{t,m}^{(\beta,\mathrm{mix})}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\pi_{t,\ell}M_{t,m}^{(\beta,\ell)}`$
is a nonnegative supermartingale under the corrected null, and
$`M_{t,\tau_{t}}^{(\beta,\mathrm{mix})}`$ is a conditionally valid
e-value at every bounded stopping time.

###### Proof of Corollary [1](#Thmcorollary1 "Corollary 1. ‣ Combining betting strategies. ‣ B.3 Mixtures ‣ Appendix B Proof of Conditional Evidence Validity ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").

For each $`\ell`$, conditional supermartingale validity gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[M_{t,i}^{(\beta,\ell)}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]\leq M_{t,i-1}^{(\beta,\ell)}.
``` |  |

The weights are nonnegative, sum to one, and are known at
$`\mathcal{H}_{t,0}=\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$. Hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[M_{t,i}^{(\beta,\mathrm{mix})}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\pi_{t,\ell}\mathbb{E}[M_{t,i}^{(\beta,\ell)}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\pi_{t,\ell}M_{t,i-1}^{(\beta,\ell)}=M_{t,i-1}^{(\beta,\mathrm{mix})}.`$ |  |

Nonnegativity is immediate, and bounded optional stopping gives the
stopped e-value claim. ∎

## Appendix C Proof of False Evolution Rate Control

###### Lemma 2.

If $`\alpha_{t}\in[0,1]`$ is
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable and $`E_{t}`$ is
conditionally valid under $`H_{t}^{0}`$, then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]\leq N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\alpha_{t},
``` |  |

where $`\alpha_{t}=0`$ is interpreted as $`A_{t}=0`$.

###### Proof.

When $`\alpha_{t}>0`$, the indicator inequality
$`\mathbf{1}\{E_{t}\geq 1/\alpha_{t}\}\leq\alpha_{t}E_{t}`$ holds. When
$`\alpha_{t}=0`$, both $`A_{t}`$ and $`\alpha_{t}E_{t}`$ are zero. Thus
$`A_{t}\leq\alpha_{t}E_{t}`$ in all cases. Since $`N_{t}^{\varepsilon}`$
and $`\alpha_{t}`$ are $`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]\leq N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\alpha_{t}\mathbb{E}[E_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]\leq N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\alpha_{t}.
``` |  |

∎

### C.1 Deterministic horizon control in Theorem [2](#Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 2. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")

###### Proof.

For $`\alpha_{t}>0`$,
Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ 4.2 E-Value Construction with Overlap Correction ‣ 4 Trustworthy Recursive Updates with Statistical Testing ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
uses $`M^{(\beta)}_{t,\tau_{t}}`$ as its evidence, with the stopping
time $`\tau_{t}\leq n`$ defined in
Proposition [1](#Thmproposition1 "Proposition 1. ‣ B.2 Stopping times ‣ Appendix B Proof of Conditional Evidence Validity ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").
The stopping extension of
Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
ensures that this evidence is conditionally valid on $`H_{t}^{0}`$.
Therefore,
Lemma [2](#Thmlemma2 "Lemma 2. ‣ Appendix C Proof of False Evolution Rate Control ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]\leq N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\alpha_{t}.
``` |  |

When $`\alpha_{t}=0`$, the algorithm sets $`A_{t}=0`$, so the same
inequality holds.

We first show that each testing level $`\alpha_{t}`$ is
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable and that the total budget
spent is at most $`q`$. At the first round, $`B_{1}=q`$ and $`R_{0}=0`$.
The baseline $`\rho_{t}^{0}=1/(T-t+1)`$ is deterministic for
$`1\leq t\leq T`$, and $`w_{t}`$ is selected using only
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$. Thus
([8](#S4.E8 "In 4.1 Testing Level Allocation with Context ‣ 4 Trustworthy Recursive Updates with Statistical Testing ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
and ([6](#S4.E6 "In 4.1 Testing Level Allocation with Context ‣ 4 Trustworthy Recursive Updates with Statistical Testing ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
make $`\rho_{t}`$, $`\alpha_{t}`$, and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
c_{t}=\frac{\alpha_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}=\min\left\{\rho_{t}B_{t},\frac{\alpha_{\max}}{R_{t-1}+1}\right\}
``` |  |

$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable. Hence the current testing
level is fixed before validation. Moreover, $`0\leq\rho_{t}\leq 1`$
implies

|     |                                         |     |
|-----|-----------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       0\leq c_{t}\leq\rho_{t}B_{t}\leq B_{t},
       ```                                      |     |

so $`B_{t+1}=B_{t}-c_{t}`$ remains nonnegative. For any $`m\leq T`$,
summing $`c_{t}=B_{t}-B_{t+1}`$ over $`t=1,\ldots,m`$ gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sum_{t=1}^{m}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}=\sum_{t=1}^{m}(B_{t}-B_{t+1})=q-B_{m+1}\leq q.
``` |  |

If $`A_{t}=1`$ and $`t\leq T`$, the total number of acceptances by
horizon $`T`$ is at least the earlier count plus the current acceptance:

|     |                      |     |
|-----|----------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       R_{T}\geq R_{t-1}+1.
       ```                   |     |

If $`A_{t}=0`$, the current false-acceptance numerator vanishes. Hence,
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}}{R_{T}\vee 1}\leq\frac{N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}.
``` |  |

Summing and taking expectations yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\operatorname{FER}_{\varepsilon}(T)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}}{R_{T}\vee 1}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\mathbb{E}[N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]}{R_{t-1}+1}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\alpha_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}\right]\leq q.`$ |  |

The third line uses that $`R_{t-1}`$ is
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable, and the fourth uses
Lemma [2](#Thmlemma2 "Lemma 2. ‣ Appendix C Proof of False Evolution Rate Control ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").
∎

### C.2 Outer stopping extension of Theorem [2](#Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 2. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")

###### Proof.

We extend the run beyond its planned horizon $`T`$ by setting
$`A_{t}=\alpha_{t}=c_{t}=0`$ for $`t>T`$ and keeping the history and
account balance fixed. Let $`\tau`$ be an almost surely finite stopping
time for $`(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\geq 0}`$. The stopped counts admit
nonnegative series representations

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
F_{\tau}^{\varepsilon}=\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}\{\tau\geq t\}N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t},\qquad R_{\tau}=\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}\{\tau\geq t\}A_{t}.
``` |  |

Only finitely many summands are nonzero on almost every path. If
$`\tau\geq t`$ and $`A_{t}=1`$, then $`R_{\tau}\geq R_{t-1}+1`$.
Consequently,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{F_{\tau}^{\varepsilon}}{R_{\tau}\vee 1}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}\{\tau\geq t\}\frac{N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}.
``` |  |

The stopping-time property implies
$`\{\tau\geq t\}=\{\tau\leq t-1\}^{c}\in\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$.
Therefore the survival indicator is predictable, and
Lemma [2](#Thmlemma2 "Lemma 2. ‣ Appendix C Proof of False Evolution Rate Control ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{1}\{\tau\geq t\}\frac{N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}\middle|\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}\right]\leq\mathbf{1}\{\tau\geq t\}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}.
``` |  |

All summands are nonnegative, so Tonelli’s theorem and the tower
property yield

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{F_{\tau}^{\varepsilon}}{R_{\tau}\vee 1}\right]`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}\{\tau\geq t\}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}\right]\leq q.`$ |  |

Since $`c_{t}=0`$ for $`t>T`$, the last inequality follows from
$`\sum_{t=1}^{T}c_{t}=q-B_{T+1}\leq q`$. This extension is stronger than
guaranteeing each fixed deterministic horizon separately: the stopping
time itself can depend on the observed recursive self-improvement
history. ∎

## Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence

### D.1 Proof of Theorem [3](#Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")

###### Proof.

Fix $`T<\infty`$ and a sequence of runs satisfying
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").
We suppress dependence of the candidates and histories on $`(N,n)`$. All
statements for sufficiently large $`(N,n)`$ are along this sequence, and
all conditional inequalities below hold almost surely.

#### Step 1: Bound the testing levels.

Equation ([7](#S4.E7 "In 4.1 Testing Level Allocation with Context ‣ 4 Trustworthy Recursive Updates with Statistical Testing ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
c_{t}=\frac{\alpha_{t}}{R_{t-1}+1}=\min\left\{\rho_{t}B_{t},\frac{\alpha_{\max}}{R_{t-1}+1}\right\}\leq\rho_{\max}B_{t}.
``` |  |

Consequently, $`B_{t+1}=B_{t}-c_{t}\geq(1-\rho_{\max})B_{t}`$. Starting
from $`B_{1}=q`$ and iterating yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
B_{t}\geq q(1-\rho_{\max})^{t-1}\geq q(1-\rho_{\max})^{T-1},\qquad t\leq T.
``` |  |

Since $`\rho_{t}\geq\rho_{\min}`$ and $`R_{t-1}+1\geq 1`$, we obtain the
deterministic lower bound

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\alpha_{t}\geq\alpha_{*}:=\min\left\{\alpha_{\max},\rho_{\min}q(1-\rho_{\max})^{T-1}\right\}>0.
``` |  | (18) |

Write $`\Lambda=\log(1/\alpha_{*})`$. Both $`\alpha_{*}`$ and
$`\Lambda`$ are independent of $`(N,n)`$.

#### Step 2: Bound exposure and the conditional mean under improvement.

At the beginning of round $`t`$, at most $`(t-1)n`$ distinct validation
indices have been exposed. An index drawn earlier in the same round has
been removed from $`\mathcal{R}_{t,i-1}`$, so it cannot contribute to
$`\mathcal{I}_{t,i-1}\cap\mathcal{R}_{t,i-1}`$. Thus, for every draw,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
0\leq\zeta_{t,i}\leq\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}:=\min\left\{1,\frac{(T-1)n}{N-n+1}\right\}.
``` |  | (19) |

Because $`T`$ is fixed and $`n/N\to 0`$, we have
$`\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}\to 0`$.

Let $`L_{t,i}`$ denote the $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable indicator
that draw $`i`$ is made. On $`\{\Delta_{t}>0,L_{t,i}=1\}`$,
Equation ([17](#A2.E17 "In Proof of Theorem . ‣ B.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix B Proof of Conditional Evidence Validity ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
and $`Y_{t,i}Q_{t,i}\geq 0`$ give

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ | $`\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}(1-Q_{t,i})\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=(1-\zeta_{t,i})\frac{1+\Delta_{t}}{2}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}}{2}.`$ |  |

Also,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}=p_{\varepsilon}+\beta\zeta_{t,i}\leq p_{\varepsilon}+\beta\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}.
``` |  |

#### Step 3: Positive log-growth bound.

For $`z\in[0,1]`$ and $`\eta\in[\eta_{-},\eta_{+}]`$, define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\phi(z,\eta):=\frac{1+z}{2}\log(1-\eta)+\frac{1-z}{2}\log\left(1-\eta+\frac{\eta}{p_{\varepsilon}+\beta z}\right).
``` |  |

On a draw, the log increment is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{t,i}:=\log\frac{M^{(\beta)}_{t,i}}{M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}}=\log\left(1-\eta_{t,i}+\frac{\eta_{t,i}Y_{t,i}}{u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}}\right).
``` |  |

Set $`z=\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}`$ and $`\bar{u}=p_{\varepsilon}+\beta z`$.
Conditional on $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$, $`\eta_{t,i}`$ is fixed. Since
$`u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}\leq\bar{u}`$ and $`Y_{t,i}\geq 0`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
V_{t,i}\geq\log\left(1-\eta_{t,i}+\frac{\eta_{t,i}Y_{t,i}}{\bar{u}}\right).
``` |  |

The function $`\log(1-\eta+\eta y/\bar{u})`$ is concave on $`[0,1]`$, so
we have:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\log\left(1-\eta+\frac{\eta y}{\bar{u}}\right)\geq(1-y)\log(1-\eta)+y\log\left(1-\eta+\frac{\eta}{\bar{u}}\right).
``` |  |

The right-hand side is increasing in $`y`$. Applying this inequality
with $`y=Y_{t,i}`$ and $`\eta=\eta_{t,i}`$, and then taking conditional
expectations, gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]\geq{}`$ | $`\displaystyle\left(1-m_{t,i}\right)\log(1-\eta_{t,i})+m_{t,i}\log\left(1-\eta_{t,i}+\frac{\eta_{t,i}}{\bar{u}}\right),`$ |  |

where $`m_{t,i}:=\mathbb{E}[Y_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}].`$ By
Step 2, on $`\{\Delta_{t}>0,\ L_{t,i}=1\}`$,

|     |                                           |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       m_{t,i}\geq\frac{1-\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}}{2}.
       ```                                        |     |

Moreover,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\log\left(1-\eta_{t,i}+\frac{\eta_{t,i}}{\bar{u}}\right)>\log(1-\eta_{t,i}),
``` |  |

so the preceding lower bound is increasing in $`m_{t,i}`$. Therefore,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[V_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]\geq{}`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1+\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}}{2}\log(1-\eta_{t,i})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{1-\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}}{2}\log\left(1-\eta_{t,i}+\frac{\eta_{t,i}}{p_{\varepsilon}+\beta\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}}\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle={}`$ | $`\displaystyle\phi(\bar{\zeta}_{N,n},\eta_{t,i}).`$ |  |

For fixed $`z`$, $`\phi(z,\eta)`$ is concave in $`\eta`$, hence its
minimum on $`[\eta_{-},\eta_{+}]`$ is attained at an endpoint. Define

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g_{N,n}:=\min\{\phi(\bar{\zeta}_{N,n},\eta_{-}),\phi(\bar{\zeta}_{N,n},\eta_{+})\}.
``` |  | (20) |

It follows that
$`\mathbb{E}[V_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]\geq g_{N,n}`$ on every
improving draw.

Continuity and $`\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}\to 0`$ imply

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g_{N,n}\longrightarrow g_{0}:=\min\{\phi(0,\eta_{-}),\phi(0,\eta_{+})\}.
``` |  |

To verify that $`g_{0}>0`$, note that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\phi(0,\eta)=\frac{1}{2}\log\left[(1-\eta)\left(1-\eta+\frac{\eta}{p_{\varepsilon}}\right)\right]
``` |  |

and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
(1-\eta)\left(1-\eta+\frac{\eta}{p_{\varepsilon}}\right)-1=\frac{\eta}{p_{\varepsilon}}\left[\varepsilon-\eta(1-p_{\varepsilon})\right].
``` |  | (21) |

Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")(c)
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
0<\eta_{-}\leq\eta\leq\eta_{+}<\frac{2\varepsilon}{1+\varepsilon}=\frac{\varepsilon}{1-p_{\varepsilon}}.
``` |  |

The right-hand side of
Equation ([21](#A4.E21 "In Step 3: Positive log-growth bound. ‣ D.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
is therefore strictly positive, so both endpoint values defining
$`g_{0}`$ are positive. Since $`\bar{\zeta}_{N,n}\to 0`$ and
$`\phi(z,\eta)`$ is continuous in $`z`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\phi(\bar{\zeta}_{N,n},\eta_{-})\to\phi(0,\eta_{-}),\qquad\phi(\bar{\zeta}_{N,n},\eta_{+})\to\phi(0,\eta_{+}).
``` |  |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
g_{N,n}=\min\{\phi(\bar{\zeta}_{N,n},\eta_{-}),\phi(\bar{\zeta}_{N,n},\eta_{+})\}\to g_{0},
``` |  |

where $`g_{0}=\min\{\phi(0,\eta_{-}),\phi(0,\eta_{+})\}>0.`$ Hence, for
all sufficiently large $`N`$ and $`n`$,

|     |                                  |     |
|-----|----------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       |g_{N,n}-g_{0}|<\frac{g_{0}}{2},
       ```                               |     |

which implies

|     |                               |     |
|-----|-------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       g_{N,n}\geq\frac{g_{0}}{2}>0.
       ```                            |     |

The log increments also lie in a fixed bounded interval. Since
$`u_{t,i}^{(\beta)}\geq p_{\varepsilon}`$, $`Y_{t,i}\in[0,1]`$, and
$`\eta_{t,i}\leq\eta_{+}`$ on draws,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\log(1-\eta_{+})\leq V_{t,i}\leq\log\left(1-\eta_{+}+\frac{\eta_{+}}{p_{\varepsilon}}\right).
``` |  |

After stopping, $`V_{t,i}=0`$, which lies in the same interval. Its
width is the fixed constant

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
h:=\log\left(\frac{1-\eta_{+}+\eta_{+}/p_{\varepsilon}}{1-\eta_{+}}\right)>0.
``` |  | (22) |

Thus the log increments are integrable, and the conditions of
Appendix [D.2](#A4.SS2 "D.2 An explicit conditional miss bound ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
hold with $`g_{t}=g_{N,n}`$ and $`h_{t}=h`$ for all sufficiently large
runs.

#### Step 4: Bound conditional miss probabilities.

Applying
Lemma [3](#Thmlemma3 "Lemma 3 (Conditional miss bound). ‣ D.2 An explicit conditional miss bound ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
with $`g_{t}=g_{N,n}`$ and $`h_{t}=h`$ gives, on $`\{\Delta_{t}>0\}`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A_{t}=0\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[-\frac{2n}{h^{2}}\left(g_{N,n}-\frac{\log(1/\alpha_{t})}{n}\right)_{+}^{2}\right]`$ |  | (23) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\exp\left[-\frac{2n}{h^{2}}\left(g_{N,n}-\frac{\Lambda}{n}\right)_{+}^{2}\right].`$ |  |  |

The second inequality follows from $`\alpha_{t}\geq\alpha_{*}`$ and
hence $`\log(1/\alpha_{t})\leq\Lambda`$.

For sufficiently large $`N`$ and $`n`$, we have $`g_{N,n}\geq g_{0}/2`$
and $`n\geq 4\Lambda/g_{0}`$. Therefore,

|     |                                               |     |
|-----|-----------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       g_{N,n}-\frac{\Lambda}{n}\geq\frac{g_{0}}{4}.
       ```                                            |     |

Set

|     |                                     |     |      |
|-----|-------------------------------------|-----|------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \kappa:=\frac{g_{0}^{2}}{8h^{2}}>0.
       ```                                  |     | (24) |

This constant is independent of $`(N,n)`$, and
Equation ([23](#A4.E23 "In Step 4: Bound conditional miss probabilities. ‣ D.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
yields

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}(A_{t}=0\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t})\leq e^{-\kappa n}\qquad\text{on }\{\Delta_{t}>0\},\quad t=1,\ldots,T.
``` |  | (25) |

In particular, the conditional miss bound is a consequence of the
sampling, allocation, and betting rules, rather than an assumed property
of the gate.

#### Step 5: TER consistency.

Let $`L_{T}^{+}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}I_{t}(1-A_{t})=U_{T}-S_{T}`$ denote the
number of improving candidates missed. Since $`I_{t}`$ is
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable,
Equation ([25](#A4.E25 "In Step 4: Bound conditional miss probabilities. ‣ D.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
and the tower property give

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[L_{T}^{+}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[I_{t}\mathbb{P}(A_{t}=0\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t})\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq e^{-\kappa n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[I_{t}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq Te^{-\kappa n}.`$ |  |

The following identity holds on every trajectory:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
1-\frac{S_{T}}{U_{T}\vee 1}=\mathbf{1}\{U_{T}=0\}+\frac{U_{T}-S_{T}}{U_{T}\vee 1}.
``` |  |

Indeed, when $`U_{T}=0`$, also $`S_{T}=0`$, and both sides equal one.
When $`U_{T}>0`$, the indicator vanishes and the equality follows from
$`U_{T}\vee 1=U_{T}`$. Taking expectations, using $`U_{T}\vee 1\geq 1`$,
and then applying
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")(a),
we obtain

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 1-\operatorname{TER}(T)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(U_{T}=0)+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{L_{T}^{+}}{U_{T}\vee 1}\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(U_{T}=0)+\mathbb{E}[L_{T}^{+}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq r_{N,n}+Te^{-\kappa n}.`$ |  |

Rearranging proves
Equation ([15](#S5.E15 "In Theorem 3. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")).
Because $`T`$ is fixed, $`r_{N,n}\to 0`$, and $`n\to\infty`$, the
right-hand side tends to zero. Since
$`0\leq\operatorname{TER}(T)\leq 1`$, we conclude that
$`\operatorname{TER}(T)\to 1`$. No independence between the future
candidate count $`U_{T}`$ and current acceptance decisions is used. ∎

### D.2 An explicit conditional miss bound

The following lemma provides the conditional miss bound used to derive
Equation ([23](#A4.E23 "In Step 4: Bound conditional miss probabilities. ‣ D.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
in Step 4 of the proof of
Theorem [3](#Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").
Since $`\eta_{t,i}<1`$, every factor in
Equation ([10](#S4.E10 "In 4.2 E-Value Construction with Overlap Correction ‣ 4 Trustworthy Recursive Updates with Statistical Testing ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
is at least $`1-\eta_{t,i}>0`$, so $`M^{(\beta)}_{t,i}>0`$ and its log
increments are well defined. For each round with $`\alpha_{t}>0`$,
define

|     |                                                            |     |
|-----|------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       V_{t,i}=\log\frac{M^{(\beta)}_{t,i}}{M^{(\beta)}_{t,i-1}},
       ```                                                         |     |

and let $`L_{t,i}`$ be the $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable indicator
that validation draw $`i`$ is made.

###### Lemma 3 (Conditional miss bound).

Suppose there exist finite $`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable
$`g_{t},h_{t}>0`$ and finite $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable interval
endpoints $`a_{t,i},b_{t,i}`$ such that, on
$`\{\Delta_{t}>0,\ L_{t,i}=1\}`$, for every $`i=1,\ldots,n`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[V_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]\geq g_{t},\qquad a_{t,i}\leq V_{t,i}\leq b_{t,i},\qquad b_{t,i}-a_{t,i}\leq h_{t}.
``` |  | (26) |

Then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}(A_{t}=0\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t})\leq\epsilon_{t}\qquad\text{on }\{\Delta_{t}>0\},
``` |  |

where

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\epsilon_{t}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\exp\left[-\frac{2n}{h_{t}^{2}}\left(g_{t}-\frac{\log(1/\alpha_{t})}{n}\right)_{+}^{2}\right],&\alpha_{t}>0,\\
1,&\alpha_{t}=0.\end{cases}
``` |  | (27) |

###### Proof.

If $`\alpha_{t}=0`$, then $`A_{t}=0`$ and $`\epsilon_{t}=1`$, so the
miss bound holds.

Work on the $`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable event
$`\{\Delta_{t}>0,\ \alpha_{t}>0\}`$ throughout the following
calculation. Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mu_{t,i}=\mathbb{E}[V_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}],\qquad Z_{t,i}=V_{t,i}-\mu_{t,i}.
``` |  |

On $`\{L_{t,i}=1\}`$,
Equation ([26](#A4.E26 "In Lemma 3 (Conditional miss bound). ‣ D.2 An explicit conditional miss bound ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
gives $`\mu_{t,i}\geq g_{t}`$, and subtracting $`\mu_{t,i}`$ from the
interval endpoints preserves their width, which is at most $`h_{t}`$. On
$`\{L_{t,i}=0\}`$, the evidence is unchanged, so $`V_{t,i}=0`$. Since
this event is $`\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}`$-measurable,
$`\mu_{t,i}=Z_{t,i}=0`$ there as well. The log increments satisfy
$`V_{t,i}\leq\log(1/p_{\varepsilon})`$ and have nonnegative conditional
means on the event under consideration, so their negative parts have
conditional expectations at most $`\log(1/p_{\varepsilon})`$. They are
therefore integrable when restricted to this event, and
$`\mathbb{E}[Z_{t,i}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]=0`$ there.

For any finite nonnegative $`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable
$`\lambda`$, the conditional Hoeffding lemma gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[e^{-\lambda Z_{t,i}}\mid\mathcal{H}_{t,i-1}]\leq\exp(\lambda^{2}h_{t}^{2}/8).
``` |  |

Both $`\lambda`$ and $`h_{t}`$ are fixed when conditioning on
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$. Dividing each factor by
$`\exp(\lambda^{2}h_{t}^{2}/8)`$ and iterating conditional expectations
from $`\mathcal{H}_{t,0}=\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$ yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left\{-\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{t,i}-\frac{n\lambda^{2}h_{t}^{2}}{8}\right\}\middle|\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}\right]\leq 1.
``` |  |

If $`A_{t}=0`$, all $`n`$ validation draws are made and the evidence
never reaches $`1/\alpha_{t}`$. Therefore,

|     |                                                                  |     |
|-----|------------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{t,i}=\log M^{(\beta)}_{t,n}<\log(1/\alpha_{t}).
       ```                                                               |     |

On this miss event, $`L_{t,i}=1`$ for every $`i=1,\ldots,n`$.
Equation ([26](#A4.E26 "In Lemma 3 (Conditional miss bound). ‣ D.2 An explicit conditional miss bound ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
then gives $`\mu_{t,i}\geq g_{t}`$ at every draw, so

|     |                                     |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \sum_{i=1}^{n}\mu_{t,i}\geq ng_{t}.
       ```                                  |     |

Define $`d_{t}=g_{t}-\log(1/\alpha_{t})/n`$. Subtracting the conditional
means from the realized log increments gives, on $`\{A_{t}=0\}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{t,i}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{t,i}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mu_{t,i}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle<\log(1/\alpha_{t})-ng_{t}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=-n\left(g_{t}-\frac{\log(1/\alpha_{t})}{n}\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=-nd_{t}.`$ |  |

Thus every outcome with $`A_{t}=0`$ satisfies this inequality, which
proves the event inclusion

|     |                                                                   |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \{A_{t}=0\}\subseteq\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{t,i}<-nd_{t}\right\}.
       ```                                                                |     |

If $`d_{t}\leq 0`$, the formula gives $`\epsilon_{t}=1`$, and the miss
probability is at most one. On $`\{d_{t}>0\}`$, take a finite positive
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable $`\lambda`$. The event
inclusion above implies that, whenever $`A_{t}=0`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\exp\left\{-\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{t,i}-\frac{n\lambda^{2}h_{t}^{2}}{8}\right\}>\exp\left(\lambda nd_{t}-\frac{n\lambda^{2}h_{t}^{2}}{8}\right).
``` |  |

The random variable on the left is nonnegative. The threshold on the
right is positive and $`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable,
since $`\lambda`$, $`d_{t}`$, and $`h_{t}`$ are all
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable. Conditional Markov’s
inequality bounds the probability of exceeding this threshold by the
conditional expectation divided by the threshold. Therefore,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A_{t}=0\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t})`$ | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left\{-\lambda\sum_{i=1}^{n}Z_{t,i}-\frac{n\lambda^{2}h_{t}^{2}}{8}\right\}\middle|\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}\right]}{\exp\left(\lambda nd_{t}-\frac{n\lambda^{2}h_{t}^{2}}{8}\right)}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\exp\left(\lambda nd_{t}-\frac{n\lambda^{2}h_{t}^{2}}{8}\right)}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\lambda nd_{t}+\frac{n\lambda^{2}h_{t}^{2}}{8}\right).`$ |  |

The second inequality uses the conditional expectation bound of one
established above by iterating the conditional Hoeffding bound. The
choice $`\lambda=4(d_{t})_{+}/h_{t}^{2}`$ is
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable and gives
$`\exp(-2nd_{t}^{2}/h_{t}^{2})`$ when $`d_{t}>0`$. Combining these cases
with $`\alpha_{t}=0`$ proves
Equation ([27](#A4.E27 "In Lemma 3 (Conditional miss bound). ‣ D.2 An explicit conditional miss bound ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")).

∎

### D.3 Extension of the power bound to mixtures

Consider the convex mixture in
Corollary [1](#Thmcorollary1 "Corollary 1. ‣ Combining betting strategies. ‣ B.3 Mixtures ‣ Appendix B Proof of Conditional Evidence Validity ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
M_{t,m}^{(\beta,\mathrm{mix})}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\pi_{t,\ell}M_{t,m}^{(\beta,\ell)},\qquad\pi_{t,\ell}\geq 0,\quad\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\pi_{t,\ell}=1,
``` |  |

where the weights are $`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable and
remain fixed during validation. Suppose at least one component
$`\ell^{\star}`$ has predictable fractions satisfying
Assumption [2](#Thmassumption2 "Assumption 2. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")(c)
on draws and weights $`\pi_{t,\ell^{\star}}\geq\pi_{*}>0`$ for every
round and run, with $`\pi_{*}`$ independent of $`(N,n)`$. Other
components need not satisfy this power condition, but they remain
nonnegative processes constructed by
Equation ([10](#S4.E10 "In 4.2 E-Value Construction with Overlap Correction ‣ 4 Trustworthy Recursive Updates with Statistical Testing ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
with predictable fractions in $`[0,1)`$.

###### Proof.

Nonnegativity gives
$`M_{t,m}^{(\beta,\mathrm{mix})}\geq\pi_{*}M_{t,m}^{(\beta,\ell^{\star})}`$.
If the mixture gate misses an improving candidate, all $`n`$ draws are
made and

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
M_{t,n}^{(\beta,\mathrm{mix})}<1/\alpha_{t},\qquad M_{t,n}^{(\beta,\ell^{\star})}<1/(\pi_{*}\alpha_{t}).
``` |  |

Steps 2–3 of the proof of
Theorem [3](#Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
apply to the selected component, with its increments held at zero after
the mixture gate stops. The conditional concentration argument in
Appendix [D.2](#A4.SS2 "D.2 An explicit conditional miss bound ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
therefore gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}(A_{t}=0\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t})\leq\exp\left[-\frac{2n}{h^{2}}\left(g_{N,n}-\frac{\log(1/(\pi_{*}\alpha_{t}))}{n}\right)_{+}^{2}\right]
``` |  |

on $`\{\Delta_{t}>0\}`$. The additional threshold penalty is at most the
fixed constant $`\log(1/\pi_{*})`$. Replacing $`\Lambda`$ by
$`\Lambda+\log(1/\pi_{*})`$ in Step 4 thus gives the same
$`e^{-\kappa n}`$ bound for all sufficiently large runs. Step 5 then
proves the same TER conclusion. Under
Assumption [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"),
the mixture remains conditionally valid by
Corollary [1](#Thmcorollary1 "Corollary 1. ‣ Combining betting strategies. ‣ B.3 Mixtures ‣ Appendix B Proof of Conditional Evidence Validity ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"),
so the proof of
Theorem [4](#Thmtheorem4 "Theorem 4. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
below also applies. Only the designated component is required to satisfy
the power condition. ∎

## Appendix E Proof of the Gain Bound

Define the cumulative loss from accepted updates by

|     |                                                 |     |
|-----|-------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       D_{T}^{-}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t}(-\Delta_{t})_{+}.
       ```                                              |     |

###### Proof of Theorem [4](#Thmtheorem4 "Theorem 4. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").

Fix a finite horizon $`T\geq 1`$ and the resource sequence and algorithm
configuration in
Appendix [D.1](#A4.SS1 "D.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").
Take $`\kappa>0`$ as in
Equation ([24](#A4.E24 "In Step 4: Bound conditional miss probabilities. ‣ D.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
and consider a sufficiently large run for
Equation ([25](#A4.E25 "In Step 4: Bound conditional miss probabilities. ‣ D.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
to hold. Throughout, the candidates, effects, and acceptance decisions
refer to the actual adaptive trajectory.

#### Step 1: Bound cumulative accepted loss.

Under
Assumption [1](#Thmassumption1 "Assumption 1. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"),
Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
and
Proposition [1](#Thmproposition1 "Proposition 1. ‣ B.2 Stopping times ‣ Appendix B Proof of Conditional Evidence Validity ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
provide conditionally valid evidence at the gate’s stopping time.
Lemma [2](#Thmlemma2 "Lemma 2. ‣ Appendix C Proof of False Evolution Rate Control ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
therefore gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]\leq N_{t}^{\varepsilon}\alpha_{t}.
``` |  |

Let $`W_{t}=(-\Delta_{t}-\varepsilon)_{+}`$. Since $`\Delta_{t}`$ is
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable and lies in $`[-1,1]`$,
$`W_{t}`$ is bounded, nonnegative, and
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable. Moreover, $`W_{t}>0`$
implies $`\Delta_{t}<-\varepsilon`$, so
$`W_{t}=N_{t}^{\varepsilon}W_{t}`$. Thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[A_{t}W_{t}]=\mathbb{E}\left[W_{t}\mathbb{E}[N_{t}^{\varepsilon}A_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]\right]\leq\mathbb{E}[\alpha_{t}W_{t}].
``` |  |

The identity

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
(-\Delta_{t})_{+}=\min\{(-\Delta_{t})_{+},\varepsilon\}+(-\Delta_{t}-\varepsilon)_{+}
``` |  |

splits each accepted loss into the part up to $`\varepsilon`$ and the
excess. Multiplying by $`A_{t}`$, summing, and taking expectations gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[D_{T}^{-}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[A_{t}\min\{(-\Delta_{t})_{+},\varepsilon\}]+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[A_{t}W_{t}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[A_{t}\min\{(-\Delta_{t})_{+},\varepsilon\}]+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\alpha_{t}(-\Delta_{t}-\varepsilon)_{+}].`$ |  |

#### Step 2: Control MDD.

For every $`0\leq r<s\leq T`$, the update rule implies

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
J(\Theta_{r})-J(\Theta_{s})=-\sum_{t=r+1}^{s}A_{t}\Delta_{t}\leq\sum_{t=r+1}^{s}A_{t}(-\Delta_{t})_{+}\leq D_{T}^{-}.
``` |  |

Taking a positive part and then maximizing over $`(r,s)`$ yields
$`\operatorname{MDD}(T)\leq D_{T}^{-}`$ pathwise. Taking expectations
and applying Step 1 gives the auxiliary bound

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{MDD}(T)]\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[A_{t}\min\{(-\Delta_{t})_{+},\varepsilon\}+\alpha_{t}(-\Delta_{t}-\varepsilon)_{+}\right].
``` |  |

This bound uses only conditional validity, not the power or candidate
discovery conditions.

#### Step 3: Bound final gain.

The update rule gives $`J(\Theta_{t})-J(\Theta_{t-1})=A_{t}\Delta_{t}`$,
and $`A_{t}\Delta_{t}=I_{t}A_{t}\Delta_{t}-A_{t}(-\Delta_{t})_{+}`$.
Summing over rounds yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
J(\Theta_{T})-J(\Theta_{0})=\sum_{t=1}^{T}A_{t}\Delta_{t}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}I_{t}A_{t}\Delta_{t}-D_{T}^{-}.
``` |  |

Since $`I_{t}\Delta_{t}=(\Delta_{t})_{+}`$ is nonnegative and
$`\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}`$-measurable, the derived conditional
miss bound in
Equation ([25](#A4.E25 "In Step 4: Bound conditional miss probabilities. ‣ D.1 Proof of Theorem ‣ Appendix D Proof of True Evolution Rate Convergence ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[I_{t}A_{t}\Delta_{t}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\Delta_{t})_{+}\mathbb{E}[A_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t}]\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq(1-e^{-\kappa n})\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{t})_{+}].`$ |  |

Summing and using
$`\mathcal{P}_{T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[(\Delta_{t})_{+}]`$, we
obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[I_{t}A_{t}\Delta_{t}]\geq(1-e^{-\kappa n})\mathcal{P}_{T}.
``` |  |

Consequently,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle G(T)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[I_{t}A_{t}\Delta_{t}]-\mathbb{E}[D_{T}^{-}]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\geq(1-e^{-\kappa n})\mathcal{P}_{T}-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[A_{t}\min\{(-\Delta_{t})_{+},\varepsilon\}+\alpha_{t}(-\Delta_{t}-\varepsilon)_{+}\right].`$ |  |

The last inequality uses Step 1. This proves
Equation ([16](#S5.E16 "In Theorem 4. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees"))
with the same $`\kappa`$ as in the proof of
Theorem [3](#Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3. ‣ 5 Theoretical Analysis ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").

Finally, the same conditional miss bound gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 0\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[(1-A_{t})(\Delta_{t})_{+}]`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[(\Delta_{t})_{+}\mathbb{P}(A_{t}=0\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{t})\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq e^{-\kappa n}\mathcal{P}_{T}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\leq Te^{-\kappa n}\longrightarrow 0,`$ |  |

since $`\mathcal{P}_{T}\leq T`$ and $`T`$ is fixed. This establishes the
vanishing missed-improvement claim accompanying the theorem. The
accepted-loss terms need not vanish. Neither the loss bound nor the
weighted miss calculation requires independence across rounds or
comparison to a different candidate trajectory. ∎

## Appendix F Harness Design with Four Components and Operators

This appendix describes the operators used in our experiments. Operator
names remain fixed across benchmarks. The specific details of the
operators are adapted to suit different benchmarks.

### F.1 State representation

Inspired by modular agent design and automated agent search in
AgentSquare and ADAS ([Shang et al., 2025](#bib.bib29); [Hu et al.,
2025](#bib.bib12)), we organize the mutable agent state into four
editable components: reasoning, routing, memory, and workflow. The
four-component representation and the specific operator definitions
below are implementation choices of our harness.

The mutable harness state is

|     |                                                           |     |
|-----|-----------------------------------------------------------|-----|
|     |
       ``` math
       \mathsf{H}=(\mathsf{R},\mathsf{P},\mathsf{M},\mathsf{W}),
       ```                                                        |     |

where $`\mathsf{R}`$ is the reasoning strategy, $`\mathsf{P}`$ is the
context router/provider, $`\mathsf{M}`$ is episodic memory, and
$`\mathsf{W}`$ is workflow. These components determine the instructions
and context for a single solver call.

A proposed transition has the structured form

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
o_{t}=(\kappa_{t},\texttt{operator},\texttt{target},\texttt{parameters}),
``` |  |

where $`\kappa_{t}\in\{\mathsf{R},\mathsf{P},\mathsf{M},\mathsf{W}\}`$
identifies the component to modify; operator specifies the operation to
perform; target identifies the rule, memory entry, or configuration
field to which the operation applies; and parameters supplies the values
or content required by that operation. For example, refining an existing
reasoning rule uses $`\kappa_{t}=\mathsf{R}`$, operator=REFINE_RULE, the
rule identifier as target, and the revised procedure in parameters.

### F.2 Reasoning operators

Motivated by prior work on modular reasoning, decomposition,
self-refinement, and prompt scope ([Zhou et al., 2023](#bib.bib46);
[Madaan et al., 2023](#bib.bib18); [Fernando et al., 2024](#bib.bib9);
[Chen et al., 2024](#bib.bib5)), we represent the reasoning strategy as
an explicit and editable rule set. The corresponding operators allow
reasoning rules and decomposition policies to be added, revised,
generalized, reprioritized, or removed based on closed historical
feedback. The particular typed rule schema and operator definitions are
implementation choices of our harness.

The reasoning component stores the base analysis protocol, decomposition
policy, and a bounded collection of specialist rules. It is compiled
into the mutable analysis instruction of the existing solver and does
not alter the fixed role prompt.

|  Operator |  Function |
|----|----|
|  ADD_SPECIALIST_RULE |  Add a local, cluster-level, or global rule with a trigger, exclusions, and executable steps. |
|  REFINE_RULE |  Rewrite only the procedure of an existing rule. |
|  SET_DECOMPOSITION_POLICY |  Set a general decomposition policy, such as identifying comparison entities before extracting their attributes. |
|  GENERALIZE_RULE_SCOPE |  Broaden a rule by relaxing its trigger, removing exclusions, or expanding scope. |
|  PROMOTE_RULE_TO_DEFAULT |  Raise a rule’s scope or priority toward default behavior. |
|  DELETE_RULE |  Remove an obsolete or harmful specialist rule. |

Table 2: Reasoning operators in the operator registry.

### F.3 Routing operators

Motivated by retrieval-augmented generation, adaptive retrieval,
evolving retrieval policies, and selective context construction ([Lewis
et al., 2020](#bib.bib14); [Asai et al., 2024](#bib.bib1); [Huang et
al., 2026](#bib.bib13); [Ye et al., 2026](#bib.bib40); [Li et al.,
2023](#bib.bib15)), we treat routing as an editable pre-solver
information-access policy. Its operators control how available context
is selected, ranked, expanded, truncated, or restored before the solver
call.

The routing component controls how the existing Wikipedia provider
selects and orders passages before the single solver call. It cannot
write memory, inspect labels from the current unfinished round, or
trigger an additional model call.

|  Operator |  Function |
|----|----|
|  SET_ROUTE_MODE |  Select full context, lexical reordering, lexical top-$`K`$, bridge reordering, or bridge top-$`K`$. |
|  REWEIGHT_RELEVANCE_SCORER |  Adjust title, body, position, and minimum-overlap relevance weights. |
|  ENABLE_BRIDGE_AWARE_EXPANSION |  Toggle expansion in which a first-hop entity raises the rank of linked second-hop evidence. |
|  SET_FIXED_TOP_K |  Cap the number of ordinary evidence passages retained by sparse routing. |
|  SET_FULL_FALLBACK |  Configure fallback to full context when sparse evidence is insufficient. |

Table 3: Routing/provider operators in the operator registry.

### F.4 Memory operators

Drawing on episodic reflection, experiential learning, reusable
procedural memory, and adaptive memory retrieval ([Shinn et al.,
2023](#bib.bib31); [Zhao et al., 2024](#bib.bib44); [Wang et al.,
2025](#bib.bib55); [Ye et al., 2026](#bib.bib40)), we treat memory as a
persistent editable experience bank whose entries can be added, revised,
selectively triggered and prioritized, or removed across evolution
rounds.

The memory component maintains a retrievable active bank. The current
protocol does not expose exact or global as proposer-selectable episodic
triggers: new episodic memories enter a common cluster-ranking path.

|  Operator |  Function |
|----|----|
|  ADD_LOCAL |  Atomically add one to three closed cases to active memory for immediate retrieval. |
|  REFINE_PROCEDURE |  Revise the steps, evidence requirement, or safe fallback used after retrieving a memory. |
|  MODIFY_TRIGGER |  Change the similarity threshold for cluster retrieval. |
|  MODIFY_PRIORITY |  Change retrieval and rendering priority. |
|  DELETE |  Move a harmful memory from the active bank to the archive. |

Table 4: Memory operators in the operator registry.

### F.5 Workflow operators

Drawing on automated and evolutionary workflow optimization ([Zhang et
al., 2025a](#bib.bib42); [Zhao et al., 2026](#bib.bib45)), we treat the
within-call execution workflow as an editable state that can evolve
across rounds through changes to verification, evidence alignment,
stopping, and fallback policies.

The workflow component specifies a plan $`\rightarrow`$ reason
$`\rightarrow`$ check $`\rightarrow`$ finalize policy within the same
completion. It does not add a second verifier or a hidden model call.

|  Operator |  Function |
|----|----|
|  SET_VERIFY_MODE |  Set the strength of internal checks before finalization. |
|  SET_EVIDENCE_ALIGNMENT |  Specify whether the answer and supporting fact identifiers must align with evidence. |
|  SET_STOP_RULE |  Specify when evidence checking stops and output begins. |
|  SET_FALLBACK_MODE |  Specify behavior under uncertainty or insufficient evidence. |

Table 5: Within-call workflow operators in the operator registry.

## Appendix G Experiment Details

### G.1 Common protocol

We evaluate Greedy, PACE, SGM-B, SGM-H, and TRUST on HotpotQA, DROP, and
APPS with Qwen-3.8-Flash and DeepSeek-V4-Flash. Each method runs for 30
outer rounds. For HotpotQA, DROP, and APPS, solver output seeds range
from 0 to 4 and data split seeds range from 42 to 46. Within each
setting, all methods share the same initial test responses. Throughout
the experiments, we reuse cached responses for identical inputs whenever
possible to ensure fair comparisons across methods. One candidate is
then selected for validation on up to 40 examples sampled without
replacement from the 200-example validation pool; validation samples may
overlap across rounds. The initial set contains 20 examples and the
validation pool contains 200 examples. All four components and 20
operators in
Appendix [F](#A6 "Appendix F Harness Design with Four Components and Operators ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees")
are available. Performance is evaluated on 300 held-out test examples,
which are used only for reporting.

In each setting, all gates start from the same initial harness, which
contains prespecified weaknesses that provide clear opportunities for
improvement. This design allows us to examine whether different gates
accept candidates that correct these weaknesses. In later rounds, when
the incumbent has typically reached a higher level of performance, we
also examine whether the gates reject subsequent candidates that could
reduce performance and cause the agent to lose earlier gains. The
experiments therefore examine different stages of the RSI process, from
repairing initial weaknesses to preserving improvements during continued
search.

For API calls, we configure the generation parameters as follows. All
roles use top-p = 1. The temperature is set to 0 for the Solver and
Allocator, and to 0.3 for the Proposer, except for HotpotQA with
DeepSeek, where the Proposer uses 0.7. The Solver’s maximum output
length is 512, 768, and 8192 tokens for HotpotQA, DROP, and APPS,
respectively, while the Allocator uses a uniform limit of 4096 tokens.
The Proposer’s maximum output length is 2400 tokens, except on HotpotQA,
where DeepSeek and Qwen use limits of 1536 and 2048 tokens,
respectively.

|  Method |  Setting |
|----|----|
|  Greedy |  Positive empirical paired gain. |
|  PACE ([Shawn, 2026](#bib.bib41)) |  Bet 0.5, candidate-level $`\alpha=0.05`$. |
|  SGM-B ([Wu et al., 2025](#bib.bib37)) |  Bernstein lower confidence bound $`>0`$, $`\delta=0.5`$, $`\alpha_{t}=\delta/(tH_{30})`$, $`H_{30}=\sum_{j=1}^{30}1/j`$. |
|  SGM-H ([Wu et al., 2025](#bib.bib37)) |  Hoeffding lower confidence bound $`>0`$, same $`\delta=0.5`$, $`\alpha_{t}=\delta/(tH_{30})`$, $`H_{30}=\sum_{j=1}^{30}1/j`$. |
|  TRUST |  $`q=0.30`$, $`\varepsilon=0.10`$, $`\beta=0.01`$, betting mixture with (0.10,0.25,0.50,0.75) |

Table 6: Gate settings in the main experiments.

We use an equally weighted mixture of four betting strategies, with
$`(\eta_{t,i}^{(1)},\eta_{t,i}^{(2)},\eta_{t,i}^{(3)},\eta_{t,i}^{(4)})=(0.10,0.25,0.50,0.75)`$
for all $`t,i`$ and mixture weights $`\pi_{t,\ell}=1/4`$ for
$`\ell=1,\ldots,4`$. The resulting statistic is
$`M_{t,m}^{(\beta,\mathrm{mix})}=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell=1}^{4}M_{t,m}^{(\beta,\ell)}`$.

### G.2 Metrics

For each seed $`b\in\{1,\ldots,B\}`$, let $`\widehat{J}_{b,t}`$ denote
the mean task score of the incumbent after round $`t`$ on
$`N_{\mathrm{test}}`$ held-out test examples, measured on the $`[0,1]`$
scale. Test results are used only for reporting. Let
$`\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{cand}}_{b,t}`$ denote the corresponding score of
the selected candidate, and define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{\Delta}_{b,t}=\widehat{J}^{\mathrm{cand}}_{b,t}-\widehat{J}_{b,t-1}.
``` |  |

Let $`\mathcal{C}_{b}`$ denote the rounds with an available selected
candidate up to round $`T`$, and let $`A_{b,t}`$ indicate acceptance. We
first compute each metric within each seed and then average across
seeds:

|  |  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\widehat{G}_{N_{\mathrm{test}}}(T)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\bigl(\widehat{J}_{b,T}-\widehat{J}_{b,0}\bigr),`$ | $`\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{MDD}}_{N_{\mathrm{test}}}(T)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\max_{0\leq r<s\leq T}\bigl(\widehat{J}_{b,r}-\widehat{J}_{b,s}\bigr)_{+},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{FER}}_{0,N_{\mathrm{test}}}(T)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\frac{\sum_{t\in\mathcal{C}_{b}}A_{b,t}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\Delta}_{b,t}\leq 0\}}{\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{C}_{b}}A_{b,t}\right)\vee 1},`$ | $`\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{TER}}_{N_{\mathrm{test}}}(T)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{B}\sum_{b=1}^{B}\frac{\sum_{t\in\mathcal{C}_{b}}A_{b,t}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\Delta}_{b,t}>0\}}{\left(\sum_{t\in\mathcal{C}_{b}}\mathbf{1}\{\widehat{\Delta}_{b,t}>0\}\right)\vee 1}.`$ |  |

We use $`N_{\mathrm{test}}=300`$, $`T=30`$, and $`B=5`$. Gain and MDD
are reported in percentage points (pp), while FER₀ and TER are reported
as percentages. FER₀ counts accepted non-improving updates, including
ties. The TER denominator counts improving selected candidates,
including those rejected by the gate. A run with no accepted updates
contributes zero to both FER₀ and TER.

### G.3 Proposer and Solver

The proposer generates three proposals using feedback from completed
rounds. We screen the candidates on 20 examples from the previous round
and select the valid candidate with the highest score for the current
round. To minimize the effect of unequal feedback sample sizes on the
proposer, we retain all 40 examples for feedback even when TRUST’s
statistical test stops early. Exposure probabilities in subsequent
rounds therefore account for the full batch rather than only the
observations up to the stopping time.

Here, we present example designs for the Proposer and Solver on
HotpotQA. The proposer template guides candidate generation using the
current harness and previously closed feedback. And the solver template
guides task execution using the current harness policies and a fixed
output contract.

### G.4 Case study for Greedy

We follow the experimental protocol in
Appendix [G.1](#A7.SS1 "G.1 Common protocol ‣ Appendix G Experiment Details ‣ Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees").
In APPS, the solver writes a program from a problem description, and the
program is evaluated using test cases. We examine a workflow update
accepted by Greedy in the main experiment with DeepSeek-V4-Flash and
seed 0. This case illustrates how Greedy can be misled by sampling noise
in a finite validation set, accepting an update that improves validation
performance while reducing performance on held-out test problems.

In round 17, the incumbent uses the following workflow.

}
````
